Follow the [tracing quickstart](https://docs.langchain.com/langsmith/trace-with-langgraph.md) to get set up.
 
 ## Why use checkpointers
 
 Checkpointers are required for the following features:
 
-* **Human-in-the-loop**: Checkpointers facilitate [human-in-the-loop workflows](/oss/python/langgraph/interrupts.md) by allowing humans to inspect, interrupt, and approve graph steps. Checkpointers are needed for these workflows as the person has to be able to view the state of a graph at any point in time, and the graph has to be able to resume execution after the person has made any updates to the state. See [Interrupts](/oss/python/langgraph/interrupts.md) for examples.
-* **Memory**: Checkpointers allow for ["memory"](/oss/python/concepts/memory.md) between interactions. In the case of repeated human interactions (like conversations) any follow up messages can be sent to that thread, which will retain its memory of previous ones. See [Add memory](/oss/python/langgraph/add-memory.md) for information on how to add and manage conversation memory using checkpointers.
-* **Time travel**: Checkpointers allow for ["time travel"](/oss/python/langgraph/use-time-travel.md), allowing users to replay prior graph executions to review and / or debug specific graph steps. In addition, checkpointers make it possible to fork the graph state at arbitrary checkpoints to explore alternative trajectories.
+* **Human-in-the-loop**: Checkpointers facilitate [human-in-the-loop workflows](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/interrupts.md) by allowing humans to inspect, interrupt, and approve graph steps. Checkpointers are needed for these workflows as the person has to be able to view the state of a graph at any point in time, and the graph has to be able to resume execution after the person has made any updates to the state. See [Interrupts](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/interrupts.md) for examples.
+* **Memory**: Checkpointers allow for ["memory"](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/concepts/memory.md) between interactions. In the case of repeated human interactions (like conversations) any follow up messages can be sent to that thread, which will retain its memory of previous ones. See [Add memory](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/add-memory.md) for information on how to add and manage conversation memory using checkpointers.
+* **Time travel**: Checkpointers allow for ["time travel"](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/use-time-travel.md), allowing users to replay prior graph executions to review and / or debug specific graph steps. In addition, checkpointers make it possible to fork the graph state at arbitrary checkpoints to explore alternative trajectories.
 * **Fault-tolerance**: Checkpointing provides fault-tolerance and error recovery: if one or more nodes fail at a given superstep, you can restart your graph from the last successful step.
 
 - **Pending writes**: When a graph node fails mid-execution at a given [super-step](#super-steps), LangGraph stores pending checkpoint writes from any other nodes that completed successfully at that super-step. When you resume graph execution from that super-step you don't re-run the successful nodes.

 ### Checkpoints
 
@@ -52,7 +52,7 @@
 
 #### Super-steps
 
-LangGraph creates a checkpoint at each **super-step** boundary. A super-step is a single "tick" of the graph where all nodes scheduled for that step execute (potentially in parallel). For a sequential graph like `START -> A -> B -> END`, there are separate super-steps for the input, node A, and node B — producing a checkpoint after each one. Understanding super-step boundaries is important for [time travel](/oss/python/langgraph/use-time-travel.md), because you can only resume execution from a checkpoint (i.e., a super-step boundary).
+LangGraph creates a checkpoint at each **super-step** boundary. A super-step is a single "tick" of the graph where all nodes scheduled for that step execute (potentially in parallel). For a sequential graph like `START -> A -> B -> END`, there are separate super-steps for the input, node A, and node B — producing a checkpoint after each one. Understanding super-step boundaries is important for [time travel](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/use-time-travel.md), because you can only resume execution from a checkpoint (i.e., a super-step boundary).
 
 In addition to super-step checkpoints, LangGraph also persists writes at the **node (task) level**. As each node within a super-step finishes, its outputs are written to the checkpointer's `checkpoint_writes` table as task entries linked to the in-progress checkpoint. These per-task writes are what enable [pending writes](#pending-writes) recovery: if another node in the same super-step fails, the successful nodes' writes are already durable and don't need to be re-run on resume. The full state snapshot is then committed once the super-step completes.

 ### Replay
 
-Replay re-executes steps from a prior checkpoint. Invoke the graph with a prior `checkpoint_id` to re-run nodes after that checkpoint. Nodes before the checkpoint are skipped (their results are already saved). Nodes after the checkpoint re-execute, including any LLM calls, API requests, or [interrupts](/oss/python/langgraph/interrupts.md) — which are always re-triggered during replay.
-
-See [Time travel](/oss/python/langgraph/use-time-travel.md) for full details and code examples on replaying past executions.
+Replay re-executes steps from a prior checkpoint. Invoke the graph with a prior `checkpoint_id` to re-run nodes after that checkpoint. Nodes before the checkpoint are skipped (their results are already saved). Nodes after the checkpoint re-execute, including any LLM calls, API requests, or [interrupts](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/interrupts.md) — which are always re-triggered during replay.
+
+See [Time travel](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/use-time-travel.md) for full details and code examples on replaying past executions.

 ### Update state
 
-You can edit the graph state using [`update_state`](https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/graphs/#langgraph.graph.state.CompiledStateGraph.update_state). This creates a new checkpoint with the updated values — it does not modify the original checkpoint. The update is treated the same as a node update: values are passed through [reducer](/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#reducers) functions when defined, so channels with reducers *accumulate* values rather than overwrite them.
-
-You can optionally specify `as_node` to control which node the update is treated as coming from, which affects which node executes next. See [Time travel: `as_node`](/oss/python/langgraph/use-time-travel#from-a-specific-node) for details.

This creates a new checkpoint with the updated values — it does not modify the original checkpoint. The update is treated the same as a node update: values are passed through [reducer](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#reducers) functions when defined, so channels with reducers *accumulate* values rather than overwrite them.
+
+You can optionally specify `as_node` to control which node the update is treated as coming from, which affects which node executes next. See [Time travel: `as_node`](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/use-time-travel#from-a-specific-node) for details.

 By default, LangGraph checkpoints write the full value of every state channel at each super-step. For long-running threads with large accumulations—such as multi-turn conversations—this can produce significant storage growth over time.

 ## Generate a query or respond
 
-With the retriever tool ready, start building the agent as a LangGraph graph. In the [Graph API](/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md), a graph is made of:
-
-* **[State](/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#state)**: Shared data that nodes read and update. This tutorial uses [`MessagesState`](/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api#messagesstate), which stores a `messages` list of [chat messages](/oss/python/langchain/messages.md).
-
-* **[Nodes](/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#nodes)**: Functions that take the current state, run a step (for example, call a model or a tool), and return state updates.
-
-* **[Edges](/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#edges)**: Connections that define which node runs next, including [conditional edges](/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#conditional-edges) that branch based on the state.
+With the retriever tool ready, start building the agent as a LangGraph graph. In the [Graph API](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md), a graph is made of:
+
+* **[State](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#state)**: Shared data that nodes read and update. This tutorial uses [`MessagesState`](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api#messagesstate), which stores a `messages` list of [chat messages](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langchain/messages.md).
+
+* **[Nodes](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#nodes)**: Functions that take the current state, run a step (for example, call a model or a tool), and return state updates.
+
+* **[Edges](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#edges)**: Connections that define which node runs next, including [conditional edges](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#conditional-edges) that branch based on the state.
 
 The first node is the agent decision point. Given the conversation so far, the model either answers the user directly or calls the retriever tool when the question needs blog context. That choice is what makes the system agentic rather than a fixed retrieve-then-generate pipeline: retrieval runs only when the model requests it.

 ## Grade documents
 
-A normal edge always sends the graph to the same next node. A [conditional edge](/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#conditional-edges) chooses the next node at runtime by running a function over the current state. +A normal edge always sends the graph to the same next node. ### [MODIFICADO] `langgraph::backward-compatibility`

- **Hash Nuevo**: `ef4bdddb21d074e4366ad13e39f64a6e98edd6ed0ea19391b98318d7bb9ffa58`

```diff
--- langgraph::backward-compatibility (prev: c349fd9a)
+++ langgraph::backward-compatibility (new: ef4bdddb)
@@ -6,7 +6,7 @@
 
 > Update LangGraph graph code in production without breaking in-flight runs.
 
-Software needs to change in production. New requirements, bug fixes, and refactors all eventually land in your graph code. Because LangGraph runs the latest deployed graph against state that has been [persisted](/oss/python/langgraph/persistence.md) for existing threads, every change you ship is effectively a backward-compatible API change with respect to your existing checkpoints.
+Software needs to change in production. New requirements, bug fixes, and refactors all eventually land in your graph code. Because LangGraph runs the latest deployed graph against state that has been [persisted](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/persistence.md) for existing threads, every change you ship is effectively a backward-compatible API change with respect to your existing checkpoints.
 
 Unlike workflow engines that pin a run to the version of code it started with, LangGraph applies the latest graph immediately to *every* thread, both new threads and threads that resume from a checkpoint. This is convenient: bug fixes propagate to in-flight conversations and agents without ceremony. It also means you must reason about how each change interacts with runs that started under the previous version of the code.
 
@@ -14,15 +14,15 @@
 
 1. [Technical compatibility](#technical-compatibility): The most common; the new code must still load and execute against existing State.
 2. [Business compatibility](#business-compatibility): Less common; existing runs should keep following the old business logic even though the code has changed.
-3. [Non-determinism](#non-determinism): Only applies to the [Functional API](/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md).
+3. [Non-determinism](#non-determinism): Only applies to the [Functional API](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md).
 
 > [!TIP]
 >
-> For a short summary of which graph topology and state changes the runtime supports by default, see [Graph migrations](/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#graph-migrations). The rest of this page covers the patterns you can apply when a change falls outside that supported set.
+> For a short summary of which graph topology and state changes the runtime supports by default, see [Graph migrations](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#graph-migrations). The rest of this page covers the patterns you can apply when a change falls outside that supported set.
 
 ## Technical compatibility
 
-Technical compatibility is the equivalent of an API breaking change in a microservice. The "API" here is the contract between your graph code and the data already persisted by the [checkpointer](/oss/python/langgraph/checkpointers.md#checkpointer-libraries) for existing threads. When a thread resumes, LangGraph deserializes the saved state, dispatches it to a node by name, and expects the node to return values that fit the state schema.
+Technical compatibility is the equivalent of an API breaking change in a microservice. The "API" here is the contract between your graph code and the data already persisted by the [checkpointer](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/checkpointers.md#checkpointer-libraries) for existing threads. When a thread resumes, LangGraph deserializes the saved state, dispatches it to a node by name, and expects the node to return values that fit the state schema.
 
 Common technical breakages:
 
@@ -30,7 +30,7 @@
 * **Renaming or removing a State key** that older checkpoints still contain or that downstream nodes still read.
 * **Tightening a State field**, such as making an `Optional` field required, narrowing a type, or adding a new required field with no default. Existing checkpoints will not satisfy the new schema.
 
-Edge topology itself is *not* persisted in the checkpoint. Adding, removing, or rerouting edges between nodes that still exist is safe for in-flight threads. Per the [Graph migrations](/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#graph-migrations) summary, the only topology change that can break an interrupted thread is renaming or removing a node.
+Edge topology itself is *not* persisted in the checkpoint. Adding, removing, or rerouting edges between nodes that still exist is safe for in-flight threads. Per the [Graph migrations](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#graph-migrations) summary, the only topology change that can break an interrupted thread is renaming or removing a node.
 
 ### Recommended patterns
 
@@ -51,15 +51,15 @@
 
 * Keep node functions tolerant of unknown keys. `TypedDict` ignores extra keys at runtime, so leftover state from an older code version will not raise unless a node explicitly reads a missing key.
 
-* Use [time travel](/oss/python/langgraph/use-time-travel.md) and [`graph.get_state`](https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/graphs/#langgraph.graph.state.CompiledStateGraph.get_state) to spot-check existing threads against the new code in a staging deployment before rolling out.
+* Use [time travel](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/use-time-travel.md) and [`graph.get_state`](https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/graphs/#langgraph.graph.state.CompiledStateGraph.get_state) to spot-check existing threads against the new code in a staging deployment before rolling out.
 
 ### Detecting in-flight threads
 
 Before you remove a node, rename a State key, or otherwise make a change that older threads cannot tolerate, you want to know whether any threads are currently parked on the version of the code you are about to drop. LangGraph itself does not maintain a search index over thread state, so the answer depends on where your graph runs.
 
-**If you deploy to [LangSmith](/langsmith/deployment.md).** Use the Agent Server's thread search to filter by status. The `status` field accepts `idle`, `busy`, `interrupted`, and `error`, so you can bulk-query for `interrupted` or `busy` threads, optionally narrowed with metadata filters. See [Filter by thread status](/langsmith/use-threads.md#filter-by-thread-status) and [List threads](/langsmith/use-threads.md#list-threads).
+**If you deploy to [LangSmith](https://docs.langchain.com/langsmith/deployment.md).** Use the Agent Server's thread search to filter by status. The `status` field accepts `idle`, `busy`, `interrupted`, and `error`, so you can bulk-query for `interrupted` or `busy` threads, optionally narrowed with metadata filters. See [Filter by thread status](https://docs.langchain.com/langsmith/use-threads.md#filter-by-thread-status) and [List threads](https://docs.langchain.com/langsmith/use-threads.md#list-threads).
 
-**Anywhere LangGraph runs.** Use [LangSmith tracing](/oss/python/langgraph/observability.md) to monitor which nodes are being entered and exited in production. This is the most reliable signal that a node or state field is no longer reachable in any active code path.
+**Anywhere LangGraph runs.** Use [LangSmith tracing](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/observability.md) to monitor which nodes are being entered and exited in production. This is the most reliable signal that a node or state field is no longer reachable in any active code path.
 
 **When you already have a `thread_id`.** Inspect that single thread directly:
 
@@ -77,7 +77,7 @@
 * Threads that have already passed `triage` should continue straight to `respond` (the old flow).
 * New threads should run the full new flow.
 
-The recommended pattern is to record the relevant *behavioral version* on the state at thread start, then branch on it with a [conditional edge](/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#conditional-edges):
+The recommended pattern is to record the relevant *behavioral version* on the state at thread start, then branch on it with a [conditional edge](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#conditional-edges):
 
 ```python theme={"theme":{"light":"catppuccin-latte","dark":"catppuccin-mocha"}}
 from typing import NotRequired
@@ -124,14 +124,14 @@
 
 ## Non-determinism
 
-This category only applies to the [Functional API](/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md) and to [**tasks**](/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md#task) or [`interrupt`](https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/types/interrupt) calls inside a [Graph API](/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md) **node**. Plain Graph API **nodes** [re-run from the start of the node function](/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#re-execution-and-idempotency) on resume; design side effects to be idempotent, but you do not need to preserve task call order unless you use **tasks** or [`interrupt`](https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/types/interrupt) in that **node**.
+This category only applies to the [Functional API](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md) and to [**tasks**](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md#task) or [`interrupt`](https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/types/interrupt) calls inside a [Graph API](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md) **node**. Plain Graph API **nodes** [re-run from the start of the node function](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#re-execution-and-idempotency) on resume; design side effects to be idempotent, but you do not need to preserve task call order unless you use **tasks** or [`interrupt`](https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/types/interrupt) in that **node**.
 
 A Functional API **entrypoint** compiles to a single **node** that replays the entrypoint body from the beginning when a run resumes, using cached [`@task`](https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/func/task) results to skip work that has already been done. Two kinds of changes break this model:
 
 * **Adding, removing, or reordering `@task` calls or [`interrupt`](https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/types/interrupt) calls** that come *before* the resume point. LangGraph matches cached results and resume values to calls by their position in the replay, so shifting that position can cause the wrong cached value to be replayed against a different call.
 * **Introducing non-deterministic operations outside of a `@task`**, such as `time.time()`, `random.random()`, or a network call inlined in the entrypoint body. On replay these produce different values than they did on the first run, which can change the control flow.
 
-For a deeper treatment with examples, see [Determinism](/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md#determinism) and [Common pitfalls](/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md#common-pitfalls) in the Functional API guide.
+For a deeper treatment with examples, see [Determinism](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md#determinism) and [Common pitfalls](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md#common-pitfalls) in the Functional API guide.
 
 If you need to make non-trivial code changes to an `@entrypoint` that has in-flight runs, the safest options are:

 <li>Upgrade <code>langchain-core</code> on the server to 1.7.0 or later, together with <code>langchain</code> 1.4.7 or later.</li>
-</ol><span data-as="p"><code>langchain</code> and <code>deepagents</code> accept any <code>langchain-core</code> 1.x, so upgrading either one can pull in 1.7.0. +</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" style="--fade-color-light:#eff1f5;--fade-color-dark:#1e1e2e"></div></div><h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="features"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#features" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="2" class="size-3 shrink-0"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Link"></use></svg></div></a></div><span class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Features</span></h3><ul>
 <li><strong>Typed answers</strong>: each interrupt carries a <code>response_schema</code> listing the decisions that call allows, so a client can build its form from the schema. See <a class="link" href="/oss/python/langchain/human-in-the-loop#read-the-response-schema">Read the response schema</a>.</li>
 <li><strong>Checked before saving</strong>: LangGraph rejects an invalid answer before it is saved, so you can correct it and answer the same interrupt again. See <a class="link" href="/oss/python/langchain/human-in-the-loop#correct-an-invalid-answer">Correct an invalid answer</a>.</li>
 <li><strong>Edits stay on the called tool</strong>: an <code>edit</code> can’t switch tools, and its arguments are checked against the tool’s own schema.</li>
 </ul><span data-as="p">Use per-call mode for new agents. LangChain plans to make it the default in the next major release. See <a class="link" href="/oss/python/langchain/human-in-the-loop#pause-once-per-tool-call">Pause once per tool call</a> and the <a class="link" href="/oss/python/concepts/human-in-the-loop">human-in-the-loop overview</a>.</span></div></div></div>

[&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">The <code>langchain.mcp</code> namespace is in beta. Importing from it raises a <code>LangChainBetaWarning</code>.</span></div></div><h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="features-2"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#features-2" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="2" class="size-3 shrink-0"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Link"></use></svg></div></a></div><span class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Features</span></h3><ul>
 <li><strong>One adapter, any transport</strong>: <code>MCPAdapter</code> infers the transport from its target — a URL, a local script over stdio, an in-process server, an <code>MCPConfig</code> dict for <a class="link" href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/connections#multiple-servers">multiple servers</a>, or a pre-built <code>fastmcp.Client</code>. <code>await adapter.list_tools()</code> returns LangChain tools ready for <code>create_agent</code>. See the <a class="link" href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp">MCP overview</a>.</li>
 <li><strong>Interrupt-driven elicitation</strong>: when a server asks for input mid-call, <code>MCPAdapter</code> surfaces the question as a LangGraph <code>interrupt()</code>, so a human answers and the run resumes. See <a class="link" href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/tools#elicitation">Elicitation</a>.</li>
 <li><strong>Authentication through FastMCP</strong>: bearer tokens, full OAuth 2.1 with dynamic client registration, or any <code>httpx.Auth</code>, including <a class="link" href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/auth#per-user-authentication">per-user authentication</a> in a deployment. See <a class="link" href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/auth">Authentication</a>.</li>
 <li><strong>Richer tool metadata</strong>: each tool carries its MCP provenance under an <code>mcp</code> namespace on its metadata, including annotations such as <code>destructive_hint</code> for <a class="link" href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/tools#human-in-the-loop">gating destructive tools behind approval</a>. See <a class="link" href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/tools#tool-metadata">Tool metadata</a>.</li>
-</ul><h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="migrating"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#migrating" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 18 18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Migrating</span></h3><span data-as="p">Moving from <code>langchain-mcp-adapters</code>? <code>MultiServerMCPClient</code> is replaced by <code>MCPAdapter</code>, and several features have changed or been removed. See the <a class="link" href="/oss/python/migrate/langchain-mcp-adapters">migration guide</a>.</span></div></div></div>

On a default-agent turn, input tokens drop <strong>65%</strong> (5,395 → 1,895), validated against our <a href="https://www.langchain.com/blog/how-we-benchmark-deep-agents" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">revamped evaluation suite</a> with no quality regression.</span><h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="optimizations"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#optimizations" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="2" class="size-3 shrink-0"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Link"></use></svg></div></a></div><span class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Optimizations</span></h3><ul>
 <li><strong>Lean prompts by default</strong>: The authored base prompt starts empty and tool-usage prose that duplicated tool schemas has been trimmed. Isolated to the default agent’s tool schemas, total description tokens drop <strong>43%</strong> (4,005 → 2,302); combined with the empty base prompt and opt-in todos, a default-agent turn’s input tokens drop <strong>65%</strong> (5,395 → 1,895). Tool behavior is unchanged. (<a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4859" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#4859</a>, <a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4979" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#4979</a>, <a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/5009" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#5009</a>)</li>
-</ul><h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="features-3"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#features-3" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 18 18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Features</span></h3><ul>
+</ul><h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="features-3"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#features-3" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="2" class="size-3 shrink-0"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Link"></use></svg></div></a></div><span class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Features</span></h3><ul>
 <li><strong><a class="link" href="/oss/python/deepagents/customization#middleware">Override a default middleware instance</a></strong>: A <code>middleware=</code> (or subagent <code>middleware</code>) instance whose <code>.name</code> matches a built-in now replaces that default in place, rather than erroring on a duplicate. For example, pass your own <code>SummarizationMiddleware(...)</code> to change the token trigger or summary model without disabling the built-in default. (<a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4251" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#4251</a>)</li>
 <li><strong>Filesystem tools</strong>: New <a class="link" href="/oss/python/deepagents/tools#built-in-harness-tools"><code>delete</code></a> tool removes a file or recursively removes a directory (<a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/3659" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#3659</a>, <a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/3851" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#3851</a>); <code>write_file</code> now overwrites an existing file instead of erroring (<a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4109" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#4109</a>); <code>FilesystemMiddleware</code> accepts a <a class="link" href="/oss/python/deepagents/overview#virtual-filesystem-access">tool allowlist</a> to expose only selected built-in tools (<a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4325" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#4325</a>, <a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4698" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#4698</a>); and reads and searches are tuned for open models — paginated <code>read_file</code> reports total and remaining lines plus the next <code>offset</code> (<a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4540" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#4540</a>), <code>grep</code>/<code>glob</code> return partial results with a <code>truncated</code> flag instead of hanging on large trees (<a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4063" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#4063</a>), and <code>grep</code> gains a 1,000-match cap with streamed output and optional context lines (<a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4570" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#4570</a>, <a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4706" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#4706</a>).</li>
 <li><strong>More prompt-caching support</strong>: Bedrock prompt caching via the <code>deepagents[aws]</code> extra (<a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/issues/4108" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#4108</a>), and automatic Fireworks prompt-cache session affinity (<a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4598" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#4598</a>).</li>
 <li><strong>NVIDIA support</strong>: A built-in Nemotron 3 Ultra harness profile plus NIM app-origin attribution. (<a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4192" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#4192</a>, <a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4455" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#4455</a>)</li>
-</ul><h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="breaking-changes-2"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#breaking-changes-2" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 18 18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Breaking changes</span></h3><ul>
+</ul><h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="breaking-changes-2"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#breaking-changes-2" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="2" class="size-3 shrink-0"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Link"></use></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Breaking changes</span></h3><ul>
 <li><strong>Planning todos are opt-in</strong>: <code>create_deep_agent</code> no longer includes <code>TodoListMiddleware</code> by default, so the <code>write_todos</code> tool, <code>todos</code> state channel, and todo-planning prompt are absent unless restored with <code>middleware=[TodoListMiddleware()]</code>. (The OpenAI Codex harness profile still opts in automatically.) (<a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4929" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#4929</a>)</li>
 <li><strong>Backend compatibility shims removed</strong>: Pass concrete <code>BackendProtocol</code> instances instead of factories, configure <code>StoreBackend</code> with an explicit <code>namespace</code>, and use the current <code>ls</code> / <code>glob</code> / <code>grep</code> / <code>ReadResult</code> APIs. Removed symbols include <code>BackendFactory</code>, <code>BACKEND_TYPES</code>, <code>FileFormat</code>, and <code>Unset</code>. New files store string <code>FileData.content</code>; older <code>list[str]</code> content stays readable and converts on next write. (<a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4541" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#4541</a>)</li>
 <li><strong>Output format changes</strong>: Empty <code>ls</code> / <code>glob</code> output is now <code>No files found</code> instead of <code>[]</code>, and <code>read_file</code> no longer renders a fixed-width <code>cat -n</code>-style gutter — update any parsers of raw tool output. (<a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4561" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">#4561</a>)</li>
-</ul><span data-as="p">Copy the following prompt into your AI coding assistant to migrate a codebase for these breaking changes:</span><div class="prompt my-6 flex flex-wrap items-center gap-x-6 gap-y-4 rounded-2xl border p-4 border-stone-100 bg-stone-50 dark:border-stone-800 dark:bg-stone-900 text-sm/[20px] text-stone-900 tracking-[-0.1px] dark:text-stone-50 print:print-color-exact"><div class="not-prose flex min-w-0 items-start gap-2" data-component-part="prompt-content-wrapper"><span aria-hidden="true" class="flex h-[1lh] shrink-0 select-none items-center justify-center" data-component-part="prompt-icon"><svg class="forced-colors:forced-color-adjust-none forced-colors:bg-[color:CanvasText]! class="size-3 shrink-0"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Link"></use></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer"><code>deepagents</code> v0.6.0</span></h2><ul>
 <li><strong><a class="link" href="/oss/python/deepagents/interpreters"><code>CodeInterpreterMiddleware</code></a></strong>: (experimental) <code>deepagents</code> now supports code execution and programmatic tool calling through a scoped QuickJS runtime.</li>
 <li>Supports <code>version=&quot;v3&quot;</code> in <code>stream_events</code> / <code>astream_events</code>. Refer to the <a class="link" href="/oss/python/deepagents/event-streaming">event streaming</a> guide for details.</li>
 <li><strong><a class="link" href="/oss/python/langgraph/pregel#deltachannel"><code>DeltaChannel</code></a> (beta)</strong> (<a href="https://www.langchain.com/blog/delta-channels-evolving-agent-runtime" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">blog</a>): Deep Agents now uses <code>DeltaChannel</code> for message history and agent files. Rather than re-serializing the full accumulated value into every checkpoint, only the incremental delta written at each step is stored — keeping checkpoint sizes small as threads grow long.</li>
-</ul><div role="note" aria-label="Warning" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-yellow-200 bg-yellow-50 dark:border-yellow-900 dark:bg-yellow-600/20 [&amp;_[data-component-part=&#x27;callout-icon&#x27;]]:mt-px" data-callout-type="warning"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 18 18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="flex-none size-5 text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300"><path d="M 9 6.75 v 1.5 m 0 3 h 0.0075 m -5.2035 3 h 10.392 c 1.155 0 1.8765 -1.2503 1.299 -2.25 L 10.299 3 c -0.5775 -0.9997 -2.0205 -0.9997 -2.598 0 L 2.505 12 c -0.5775 0.9997 0.144 2.25 1.299 2.25 z" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"></path></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p"><strong>Rolling back from v0.6.0 is not supported once threads have persisted.</strong> Deep Agents v0.6.0 changes persisted message history and agent files to <code>DeltaChannel</code>, which writes checkpoints in a new format that earlier versions cannot read. Downgrading to an earlier Deep Agents version switches these channels back to non-delta channels, leaving existing delta checkpoints unreadable and causing incomplete or incorrect state reconstruction. If you need to roll back, use the <a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/langgraph/tree/main/examples/delta-channel-dump" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">delta-channel-dump recovery script</a> to migrate affected threads, or discard them, before downgrading. More generally, avoid switching a persisted channel between delta and non-delta representations. See <a class="link" href="/oss/python/langgraph/pregel#version-compatibility-and-rollbacks">Version compatibility and channel changes</a>.</span></div></div><ul>
+</ul><div role="note" aria-label="Warning" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-yellow-200 bg-yellow-50 dark:border-yellow-900 dark:bg-yellow-600/20 [&amp;_[data-component-part=&#x27;callout-icon&#x27;]]:mt-px" data-callout-type="warning"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="flex-none size-5 text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutWarning"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p"><strong>Rolling back from v0.6.0 is not supported once threads have persisted.</strong> Deep Agents v0.6.0 changes persisted message history and agent files to <code>DeltaChannel</code>, which writes checkpoints in a new format that earlier versions cannot read. Downgrading to an earlier Deep Agents version switches these channels back to non-delta channels, leaving existing delta checkpoints unreadable and causing incomplete or incorrect state reconstruction. If you need to roll back, use the <a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/langgraph/tree/main/examples/delta-channel-dump" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">delta-channel-dump recovery script</a> to migrate affected threads, or discard them, before downgrading. More generally, avoid switching a persisted channel between delta and non-delta representations. See <a class="link" href="/oss/python/langgraph/pregel#version-compatibility-and-rollbacks">Version compatibility and channel changes</a>.</span></div></div><ul>
 <li><strong><a class="link" href="/oss/python/deepagents/profiles">Harness profiles</a></strong>: Register per-provider or per-model configuration bundles (<code>HarnessProfile</code>) that <code>create_deep_agent</code> applies automatically when a model is selected — system-prompt tweaks, tool overrides, middleware changes, and subagent defaults — without modifying the call site.</li>
 <li><strong><a class="link" href="/oss/python/deepagents/backends#contexthubbackend"><code>ContextHubBackend</code></a></strong> (<a href="https://www.langchain.com/blog/introducing-context-hub" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">blog</a>): A new filesystem backend backed by LangSmith Hub. Agent files — skills, memories, and other persisted context — are stored as Hub commits, giving you version history on every write and LangSmith-native durability without provisioning a separate LangGraph store.</li>
 </ul></div></div></div>

(v3) with typed, per-channel projections.</span><ul>
 <li>
 <span data-as="p"><strong><a class="link" href="/oss/python/langgraph/pregel#deltachannel"><code>DeltaChannel</code></a> (beta)</strong>: A new channel type that stores only the incremental delta at each step rather than re-serializing the full accumulated value. Most useful for channels that grow large over time, for example a message list in a long-running thread. Use <code>snapshot_frequency=K</code> to write a full snapshot every K steps and bound read latency.</span>
 </li>
@@ -1300,7 +1300,7 @@
 <span data-as="p"><strong>New event streaming API (beta)</strong>: Pass <code>version=&quot;v3&quot;</code> to <code>stream_events()</code> / <code>astream_events()</code> for a content-block-centric protocol with typed, per-channel projections (<code>run.values</code>, <code>run.messages</code>, <code>run.lifecycle</code>, <code>run.subgraphs</code>) plus opt-in transformers for updates, custom events, checkpoints, tasks, and debug. <code>run.messages</code> yields one <code>ChatModelStream</code> per LLM call with typed sub-projections for text, reasoning, tool calls, and usage. <code>version=&quot;v1&quot;</code> and <code>version=&quot;v2&quot;</code> are unchanged.</span>
 </li>
 </ul><span data-as="p">Timeouts and error handlers are Python-only; retry policies continue to work in both Python and TypeScript.</span></div></div></div>

- **Hash Nuevo**: `cc807665512d8b83b1d1844e4cf27ddefffd40c4c8a4987e0c53a1ed32013197`

```diff
--- langgraph::deploy (prev: 6859fd21)
+++ langgraph::deploy (new: cc807665)
@@ -6,11 +6,11 @@
 
 > Deploy LangGraph agents to production with LangSmith Cloud or JavaScript frameworks and hosting platforms.
 
-When you are ready to deploy your LangGraph agent to production, choose a hosting model that fits your stack. **[LangSmith Cloud](/langsmith/deploy-to-cloud.md)** provides fully managed infrastructure for stateful, long-running agents with persistent state and background execution.
+When you are ready to deploy your LangGraph agent to production, choose a hosting model that fits your stack. **[LangSmith Cloud](https://docs.langchain.com/langsmith/deploy-to-cloud.md)** provides fully managed infrastructure for stateful, long-running agents with persistent state and background execution.
 
 > [!TIP]
 >
-> LangSmith offers multiple deployment options beyond Cloud, including [hybrid](/langsmith/hybrid.md), [standalone servers](/langsmith/deploy-standalone-server.md), and [self-hosted with control plane](/langsmith/deploy-with-control-plane.md). For more information, see the [LangSmith Deployment overview](/langsmith/deployment.md).
+> LangSmith offers multiple deployment options beyond Cloud, including [hybrid](https://docs.langchain.com/langsmith/hybrid.md), [standalone servers](https://docs.langchain.com/langsmith/deploy-standalone-server.md), and [self-hosted with control plane](https://docs.langchain.com/langsmith/deploy-with-control-plane.md). For more information, see the [LangSmith Deployment overview](https://docs.langchain.com/langsmith/deployment.md).
 
 ## LangSmith Cloud
 
@@ -27,7 +27,7 @@
 
 #### 1. Create a repository on GitHub
 
-Your application's code must reside in a GitHub repository to be deployed on LangSmith. Both public and private repositories are supported. For this quickstart, first make sure your app is LangGraph-compatible by following the [local server setup guide](/oss/python/langgraph/studio.md#set-up-local-agent-server). Then, push your code to the repository.
+Your application's code must reside in a GitHub repository to be deployed on LangSmith. Both public and private repositories are supported. For this quickstart, first make sure your app is LangGraph-compatible by following the [local server setup guide](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/studio.md#set-up-local-agent-server). Then, push your code to the repository.
 
 #### 2. Deploy to LangSmith

@@ -7,7 +7,7 @@
 A LangGraph [`StateGraph`](https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/graph/state/StateGraph) received concurrent updates to its state from multiple nodes to a state property that doesn't
 support it.
 
-One way this can occur is if you are using a [fanout](/oss/python/langgraph/use-graph-api.md#map-reduce-and-the-send-api)
+One way this can occur is if you are using a [fanout](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/use-graph-api.md#map-reduce-and-the-send-api)
 or other parallel execution in your graph and you have defined a graph like this:
 
 ```python theme={"theme":{"light":"catppuccin-latte","dark":"catppuccin-mocha"}}

class="link" href="/oss/javascript/langchain/event-streaming">event streaming</a>, tool-call arguments now stream as <code>tool-call-delta</code> events. Each event carries only the new part of the arguments, as <code>text-delta</code> does for text. Earlier versions sent <code>block-delta</code> events that repeated all of the arguments so far.</span><h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="upgrade-clients-before-the-server"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#upgrade-clients-before-the-server" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 18 18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Upgrade clients before the server</span></h3><span data-as="p">Clients older than the versions below do not handle <code>tool-call-delta</code>. For example, earlier versions of <code>@langchain/react</code> can stop updating a <code>useMessages</code> view. The <a class="link" href="/langsmith/agent-server">Agent Server</a> passes events through unchanged, so the package versions on the server decide which events clients receive.</span><ol>

class="cursor-pointer"><code>@langchain/core</code> v1.3.0</span></h2><span data-as="p">In <a class="link" href="/oss/javascript/langchain/event-streaming">event streaming</a>, tool-call arguments now stream as <code>tool-call-delta</code> events. Each event carries only the new part of the arguments, as <code>text-delta</code> does for text. Earlier versions sent <code>block-delta</code> events that repeated all of the arguments so far.</span><h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="upgrade-clients-before-the-server"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#upgrade-clients-before-the-server" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="2" class="size-3 shrink-0"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Link"></use></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Upgrade clients before the server</span></h3><span data-as="p">Clients older than the versions below do not handle <code>tool-call-delta</code>. For example, earlier versions of <code>@langchain/react</code> can stop updating a <code>useMessages</code> view. The <a class="link" href="/langsmith/agent-server">Agent Server</a> passes events through unchanged, so the package versions on the server decide which events clients receive.</span><ol>
 <li>In every client that reads the stream, upgrade <code>@langchain/react</code>, <code>@langchain/vue</code>, <code>@langchain/svelte</code>, or <code>@langchain/angular</code> to 1.2.6 or later.</li>
 <li>Upgrade <code>@langchain/core</code> on the server to 1.3.0 or later. Also upgrade each of these provider packages the server uses, because earlier versions still send <code>block-delta</code>:
 <ul>
@@ -1250,14 +1250,14 @@
 </ul>
 </li>
 </ol><span data-as="p">Upgrading <code>langchain</code>, <code>deepagents</code>, or a provider package can pull in <code>@langchain/core</code> 1.3.0. To hold the server back until you upgrade every client, pin <code>@langchain/core</code> to <code>&lt;1.3.0</code> in <code>package.json</code>. The provider versions in step 2 require <code>@langchain/core</code> 1.3.0, so you cannot install them while the pin is in place.</span></div></div></div>

+</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" style="--fade-color-light:#eff1f5;--fade-color-dark:#1e1e2e"></div></div><h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="features"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#features" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="2" class="size-3 shrink-0"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Link"></use></svg></div></a></div><span class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Features</span></h3><ul>
 <li><strong>Typed answers</strong>: each interrupt carries a <code>response_schema</code> listing the decisions that call allows, so a client can build its form from the schema. See <a class="link" href="/oss/javascript/langchain/human-in-the-loop#read-the-response-schema">Read the response schema</a>.</li>
 <li><strong>Checked before saving</strong>: LangGraph rejects an invalid answer before it is saved, so you can correct it and answer the same interrupt again. See <a class="link" href="/oss/javascript/langchain/human-in-the-loop#correct-an-invalid-answer">Correct an invalid answer</a>.</li>
 <li><strong>Edits stay on the called tool</strong>: an <code>edit</code> can’t switch tools, and its arguments are checked against the tool’s own schema.</li>
 <li><strong><code>respond</code> everywhere</strong>: <code>interruptOn: { tool: true }</code> now allows every decision, <code>respond</code> included, in both modes, as in Python.</li>
 </ul><span data-as="p">Use per-call mode for new agents. LangChain plans to make it the default in the next major release. See <a class="link" href="/oss/javascript/langchain/human-in-the-loop#pause-once-per-tool-call">Pause once per tool call</a> and the <a class="link" href="/oss/javascript/concepts/human-in-the-loop">human-in-the-loop overview</a>.</span></div></div></div>

 <li><strong>ReducedValue</strong>: Define fields with custom reducers for accumulating values. Supports separate input and output schemas for type-safe reducer inputs.</li>
 <li><strong>UntrackedValue</strong>: Define transient state that exists during execution but is never checkpointed - useful for database connections, caches, or runtime-only configuration.</li>
 <li><strong>MessagesValue</strong>: A prebuilt <code>ReducedValue</code> for chat messages with the standard messages reducer.</li>

See [Lifecycle](#lifecycle) for more information.

 ```py theme={"theme":{"light":"catppuccin-latte","dark":"catppuccin-mocha"}}

@@ -519,15 +519,15 @@
 
 LangGraph defines the streaming primitives. For using streaming with LangChain or Deep Agents, review the relevant product docs:
 
-* [LangChain agent streaming](/oss/python/langchain/event-streaming.md) covers ReAct-style agent messages, tool calls, and middleware updates.
-* [Deep Agents streaming](/oss/python/deepagents/event-streaming.md) covers subagents, nested messages, and subagent tool calls.
-* [LangChain frontend patterns](/oss/python/langchain/frontend/overview.md) and [LangGraph frontend patterns](/oss/python/langgraph/frontend/overview.md) show UI use cases built on top of streamed state.

 The wire-level event and command formats are defined in the [Agent Protocol](https://github.com/langchain-ai/agent-protocol) repository and consumable as [`langchain-protocol`](https://pypi.org/project/langchain-protocol/) on PyPI and [`@langchain/protocol`](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@langchain/protocol) on npm.

For stable, name-based namespaces, wrap each subgraph with a unique node name — see [parallel subgraphs](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/use-subgraphs.md#subgraph-persistence).

### [MODIFICADO] `langgraph::functional-api`
- **URL Canónica**: https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api
- **Hash Anterior**: `e7b9a80879f0f575468e85dd4bbabc43e19ac9da8a46e215a68a6797a581b6a4`
- **Hash Nuevo**: `9078f012db765853977a8495c23f0555430968576f856e1f90a2d12102b449a4`

```diff
--- langgraph::functional-api (prev: e7b9a808)
+++ langgraph::functional-api (new: 9078f012)
@@ -4,7 +4,7 @@
 
 # Functional API overview
 
-The **Functional API** allows you to add LangGraph's key features ([persistence](/oss/python/langgraph/persistence.md), [memory](/oss/python/langgraph/add-memory.md), [human-in-the-loop](/oss/python/langgraph/interrupts.md), and [streaming](/oss/python/langgraph/streaming.md)) to your applications with minimal changes to your existing code.
+The **Functional API** allows you to add LangGraph's key features ([persistence](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/persistence.md), [memory](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/add-memory.md), [human-in-the-loop](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/interrupts.md), and [streaming](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/streaming.md)) to your applications with minimal changes to your existing code.
 
 It is designed to integrate these features into existing code that may use standard language primitives for branching and control flow, such as `if` statements, `for` loops, and function calls. Unlike many data orchestration frameworks that require restructuring code into an explicit pipeline or DAG, the Functional API allows you to incorporate these capabilities without enforcing a rigid execution model.
 
@@ -17,22 +17,22 @@
 
 > [!TIP]
 >
-> For information on how to use the functional API, see [Use Functional API](/oss/python/langgraph/use-functional-api.md).
+> For information on how to use the functional API, see [Use Functional API](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/use-functional-api.md).
 
 ## Functional API vs. Graph API
 
-For users who prefer a more declarative approach, LangGraph's [Graph API](/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md) allows you to define workflows using a Graph paradigm. Both APIs share the same underlying runtime, so you can use them together in the same application.
+For users who prefer a more declarative approach, LangGraph's [Graph API](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md) allows you to define workflows using a Graph paradigm. Both APIs share the same underlying runtime, so you can use them together in the same application.
 
 Here are some key differences:
 
 * **Control flow**: The Functional API does not require thinking about graph structure. You can use standard Python constructs to define workflows. This will usually trim the amount of code you need to write.
-* **Short-term memory**: The **GraphAPI** requires declaring a [**State**](/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#state) and may require defining [**reducers**](/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#reducers) to manage updates to the graph state. `@entrypoint` and `@tasks` do not require explicit state management as their state is scoped to the function and is not shared across functions.
-* **Checkpointing**: Both APIs generate and use checkpoints. In the **Graph API** a new checkpoint is generated after every [superstep](/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md). In the **Functional API**, when tasks are executed, their results are saved to an existing checkpoint associated with the given entrypoint instead of creating a new checkpoint.
+* **Short-term memory**: The **GraphAPI** requires declaring a [**State**](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#state) and may require defining [**reducers**](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#reducers) to manage updates to the graph state. `@entrypoint` and `@tasks` do not require explicit state management as their state is scoped to the function and is not shared across functions.
+* **Checkpointing**: Both APIs generate and use checkpoints. In the **Graph API** a new checkpoint is generated after every [superstep](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md). In the **Functional API**, when tasks are executed, their results are saved to an existing checkpoint associated with the given entrypoint instead of creating a new checkpoint.
 * **Visualization**: The Graph API makes it easy to visualize the workflow as a graph which can be useful for debugging, understanding the workflow, and sharing with others. The Functional API does not support visualization as the graph is dynamically generated during runtime.

 * **Checkpointing**: When you need to save the result of a long-running operation to a checkpoint, so you don't need to recompute it when resuming the workflow.
 * **Human-in-the-loop**: If you're building a workflow that requires human intervention, you MUST use **tasks** to encapsulate any randomness (e.g., API calls) to ensure that the workflow can be resumed correctly. See the [determinism](#determinism) section for more details.
 * **Parallel Execution**: For I/O-bound tasks, **tasks** enable parallel execution, allowing multiple operations to run concurrently without blocking (e.g., calling multiple APIs).
-* **Observability**: Wrapping operations in **tasks** provides a way to track the progress of the workflow and monitor the execution of individual operations using [LangSmith](/langsmith/observability.md).
+* **Observability**: Wrapping operations in **tasks** provides a way to track the progress of the workflow and monitor the execution of individual operations using [LangSmith](https://docs.langchain.com/langsmith/observability.md).
 * **Retryable Work**: When work needs to be retried to handle failures or inconsistencies, **tasks** provide a way to encapsulate and manage the retry logic.

 ### Re-execution and idempotency
 
-When you compile with a [checkpointer](/oss/python/langgraph/persistence.md), LangGraph saves checkpoints at [super-step](#graphs) boundaries, not mid-function inside a node. If execution stops and later resumes (for example after an [interrupt](/oss/python/langgraph/interrupts.md) or a [retry](/oss/python/langgraph/fault-tolerance.md#retries)), the affected **node** runs again from the start of its function. Code and side effects before the pause run again.
-
-**Idempotency.** Design **node** logic so re-execution does not corrupt state. If a node inserts a database row, running it twice should not create duplicate rows unless that is intentional. Use idempotency keys, upserts, or read-before-write checks. For effects around `interrupt()`, see [Side effects called before `interrupt` must be idempotent](/oss/python/langgraph/interrupts#side-effects-called-before-interrupt-must-be-idempotent).
-
-**Graph changes.** [Determinism](/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md#determinism) rules about code changes do not apply to graph structure. You can add or remove **nodes** and edges without breaking resume for existing threads. Resumed runs use saved state and execute whatever graph you compile now.
-

LangGraph restores completed **task** results from the checkpointer, but changing **task** or [`interrupt`](https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/types/interrupt) order in code before the resume point can mismatch cached values. A [Functional API](/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md) **entrypoint** compiles to a single **node** that runs the whole entrypoint method this way. Code and side effects before the pause run again.
+
+**Idempotency.** Design **node** logic so re-execution does not corrupt state. If a node inserts a database row, running it twice should not create duplicate rows unless that is intentional. Use idempotency keys, upserts, or read-before-write checks. For effects around `interrupt()`, see [Side effects called before `interrupt` must be idempotent](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/interrupts#side-effects-called-before-interrupt-must-be-idempotent).
+
+**Graph changes.** [Determinism](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md#determinism) rules about code changes do not apply to graph structure. You can add or remove **nodes** and edges without breaking resume for existing threads. Resumed runs use saved state and execute whatever graph you compile now.
+
+**Tasks and interrupts inside a node.** If a **node** calls [**tasks**](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md#task) or [`interrupt`](https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/types/interrupt), stricter determinism rules apply on resume. LangGraph restores completed **task** results from the checkpointer, but changing **task** or [`interrupt`](https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/types/interrupt) order in code before the resume point can mismatch cached values. A [Functional API](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md) **entrypoint** compiles to a single **node** that runs the whole entrypoint method this way. See [Determinism](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md#determinism), [Idempotency](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md#idempotency), and [Using tasks in nodes](#using-tasks-in-nodes).

-If a [node](#nodes) contains multiple operations, you may find it easier to implement each operation as a [**task**](/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md#task) instead of splitting the logic across multiple nodes. ### [MODIFICADO] `langgraph::interrupts`
- **URL Canónica**: https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/interrupts
- **Hash Anterior**: `909d0534a2f35b3ad19f03b0f326abe9cd6dbf547b0ec50c41b5fb1abed10ec5`
- **Hash Nuevo**: `8281a54d1a55d2a4a34faa049eb0e124b2ebd70aff04ac5247245b593c720aee`

```diff
--- langgraph::interrupts (prev: 909d0534)
+++ langgraph::interrupts (new: 8281a54d)
@@ -4,7 +4,7 @@
 
 # Interrupts
 
-Interrupts allow you to pause graph execution at specific points and wait for external input before continuing. This enables human-in-the-loop patterns where you need external input to proceed. When an interrupt is triggered, LangGraph saves the graph state using its [persistence](/oss/python/langgraph/persistence.md) layer and waits indefinitely until you resume execution.
+Interrupts allow you to pause graph execution at specific points and wait for external input before continuing. This enables human-in-the-loop patterns where you need external input to proceed. When an interrupt is triggered, LangGraph saves the graph state using its [persistence](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/persistence.md) layer and waits indefinitely until you resume execution.
 
 Interrupts work by calling the `interrupt()` function at any point in your graph nodes. The function accepts any JSON-serializable value which is surfaced to the caller. When you're ready to continue, you resume execution by re-invoking the graph using `Command`, which then becomes the return value of the `interrupt()` call from inside the node.
 
@@ -12,7 +12,7 @@
 
 * **Checkpointing keeps your place:** the checkpointer writes the exact graph state so you can resume later, even when in an error state.
 * **`thread_id` is your pointer:** set `config={"configurable": {"thread_id": ...}}` to tell the checkpointer which state to load.
-* **Interrupt payloads surface via `stream.interrupts`:** when using [event streaming](/oss/python/langgraph/event-streaming.md) (`graph.stream_events(..., version="v3")`), the values you pass to `interrupt()` appear on `stream.interrupts`, and `stream.interrupted` is `True` when the run pauses for input.

### [MODIFICADO] `langgraph::persistence`
- **URL Canónica**: https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/persistence
- **Hash Anterior**: `70aaea872d68bf47da931b65dca03abc0c274c31caebecd5f730d5b2b5d50cd8`

```diff
--- langgraph::persistence (prev: 70aaea87)
+++ langgraph::persistence (new: 9985e697)
@@ -10,10 +10,10 @@
 
 LangGraph provides two complementary persistence systems:
 
-* **[Checkpointers](/oss/python/langgraph/checkpointers.md)** persist a thread's graph state as checkpoints. Use them for short-term, thread-scoped memory, including conversation continuity, human-in-the-loop workflows, time travel, and fault tolerance.
-* **[Stores](/oss/python/langgraph/stores.md)** persist application-defined data outside the graph state. Use them for long-term, cross-thread memory, including user preferences, facts, and shared knowledge.
+* **[Checkpointers](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/checkpointers.md)** persist a thread's graph state as checkpoints. Use them for short-term, thread-scoped memory, including conversation continuity, human-in-the-loop workflows, time travel, and fault tolerance.
+* **[Stores](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/stores.md)** persist application-defined data outside the graph state. Use them for long-term, cross-thread memory, including user preferences, facts, and shared knowledge.
 
-Most applications can use both: a [checkpointer](/oss/python/langgraph/checkpointers.md) tracks the current thread, and a [store](/oss/python/langgraph/stores.md) tracks durable information across threads.
+Most applications can use both: a [checkpointer](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/checkpointers.md) tracks the current thread, and a [store](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/stores.md) tracks durable information across threads.
 
 ## Quickstart
 
@@ -37,7 +37,7 @@
 > [!NOTE]
 >
 > **Agent Server handles persistence automatically**
-> When using the [Agent Server](/langsmith/agent-server.md), you do not need to implement or configure checkpointers or stores manually. The server handles persistence infrastructure behind the scenes.
+> When using the [Agent Server](https://docs.langchain.com/langsmith/agent-server.md), you do not need to implement or configure checkpointers or stores manually. The server handles persistence infrastructure behind the scenes.
 
 ## Checkpointer vs. store
 
@@ -48,7 +48,7 @@
 | Memory type | Short-term, thread-scoped memory | Long-term, cross-thread memory |
 | Use for | Conversation continuity, human-in-the-loop, time travel, and fault tolerance | User preferences, facts, and shared knowledge |
 | Access pattern | Pass a `thread_id` in graph config | Read and write items from nodes or application code |
-| Full guide | [Checkpointers](/oss/python/langgraph/checkpointers.md) | [Stores](/oss/python/langgraph/stores.md) |
+| Full guide | [Checkpointers](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/checkpointers.md) | [Stores](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/stores.md) |
 
 ## Troubleshooting common issues
 
@@ -90,15 +90,15 @@
 
 When a subgraph updates state, the parent graph may not see the changes immediately. This is because each subgraph manages its own checkpoint namespace.
 
-**Fix:** Use [shared state via Store](/oss/python/langgraph/stores.md) for data that needs to cross graph boundaries, or configure your subgraph to write to the parent checkpoint.
+**Fix:** Use [shared state via Store](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/stores.md) for data that needs to cross graph boundaries, or configure your subgraph to write to the parent checkpoint.
 
 ## Next steps
 
-* [Use checkpointers](/oss/python/langgraph/checkpointers.md) to persist and inspect thread state.
-* [Use stores](/oss/python/langgraph/stores.md) to persist durable data across threads.

+* [Use stores](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/stores.md) to persist durable data across threads.

 Trusted by companies shaping the future of agents—including Klarna, Uber, J.P. Morgan, and more—LangGraph is a low-level orchestration framework and runtime for building, managing, and deploying long-running, stateful agents. LangGraph gives you fine-grained control to mix deterministic, hand-coded steps with LLM-driven agentic steps in the same graph, so you can build bespoke agents that behave exactly the way your application requires.
 
-LangGraph is very low-level, and focused entirely on agent **orchestration**. Before using LangGraph, we recommend you familiarize yourself with some of the components used to build agents, starting with [models](/oss/python/langchain/models.md) and [tools](/oss/python/langchain/tools.md).
+LangGraph is very low-level, and focused entirely on agent **orchestration**. -We will commonly use [LangChain](/oss/python/langchain/overview.md) components throughout the documentation to integrate models and tools, but you don't need to use LangChain to use LangGraph. If you are just getting started with agents or want a higher-level abstraction, we recommend you use LangChain's [agents](/oss/python/langchain/agents.md) that provide prebuilt architectures for common LLM and tool-calling loops.
+We will commonly use [LangChain](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langchain/overview.md) components throughout the documentation to integrate models and tools, but you don't need to use LangChain to use LangGraph. If you are just getting started with agents or want a higher-level abstraction, we recommend you use LangChain's [agents](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langchain/agents.md) that provide prebuilt architectures for common LLM and tool-calling loops.
 
 LangGraph is focused on the underlying capabilities important for agent orchestration: durable execution, streaming, human-in-the-loop, and more.

-* [LangChain](/oss/python/langchain/overview.md) is the agent framework: abstractions and integrations for models, tools, and agent loops.
